STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY — preserve these established static environment facts:
The Stone Hearth Tavern is a rectangular stone hall measuring eighty feet east‑west and sixty feet north‑south, with thick stone walls that creak under the wooden floor. The south wall hosts the main entrance: a single back door centered on the wall, its wooden latch protruding from the rear to allow inward swing. A clear aisle at least twelve feet wide runs from this doorway straight north toward the hall’s northern edge. Along the east side of the hall sits the counter—a three‑foot‑wide bar that stretches twenty feet in length. Its centreline lies ten feet north of the south wall and fifteen feet west of the eastern wall, leaving ample space for patrons to move around. The polished tables are set along this counter; each is four by four feet and fixed at their positions. Near the back end of the counter a high bar stool stands beside a shelf of crystal cups that rises six feet tall and three feet deep. On the west wall sits the hearth, occupying a ten‑by‑twelve‑foot stone area centred fifteen feet northward from the south wall. Beside this hearth an upright barrel—eight feet high with a four‑foot diameter—is positioned on the floor. The rear northwest corner of the hall holds a single back table measuring five by five feet; it is placed three feet north‑west of the counter’s centreline, ensuring no overlap with other furniture or walls. Above each polished table and above the back table hangs a lantern suspended six feet from the floor, providing illumination for patrons. All objects are fixed in place except the wooden latch on the entrance door, which can be moved to open or close the hall. The layout preserves the medieval tavern’s authentic spatial arrangement with stone walls, creaking floors, and ample room for movement around the counter and hearth.

A goblin with green skin leans over the counter, its tiny hands clutching a chipped mug; Amy refills it from a barrel beside the hearth.

From the doorway, a dragon‑shaped creature slides onto a high bar stool, scales glittering like polished obsidian. Its wings unfurl to reveal a pair of amber eyes that scan the room. Amy greets the newcomer with a warm smile and lifts a crystal cup from her shelf, pouring a special brew into it before handing it over.

The dragon licks its lips, then takes a slow sip, letting the liquid swirl across its tongue. Amy watches as the creature exhales contentedly; she wipes the bar with a fresh cloth, smoothing out any drips of ale that have fallen onto the wood.

Finally, Amy turns off the lanterns at each table and pulls a wooden latch on the back door. She steps outside to close it, locking the tavern for the night as the moon rises over the medieval town.

A clear aisle at least twelve feet wide runs from this doorway straight north toward the hall’s northern edge. Along the east side of the hall sits the counter—a three‑foot‑wide bar that stretches twenty feet in length. Its centreline lies ten feet north of the south wall and fifteen feet west of the eastern wall, leaving ample space for patrons to move around. The polished tables are set along this counter; each is four by four feet and fixed at their positions. Near the back end of the counter a high bar stool stands beside a shelf of crystal cups that rises six feet tall and three feet deep. On the west wall sits the hearth, occupying a ten‑by‑twelve‑foot stone area centred fifteen feet northward from the south wall. Beside this hearth an upright barrel—eight feet high with a four‑foot diameter—is positioned on the floor. The rear northwest corner of the hall holds a single back table measuring five by five feet; it is placed three feet north‑west of the counter’s centreline, ensuring no overlap with other furniture or walls. Above each polished table and above the back table hangs a lantern suspended six feet from the floor, providing illumination for patrons. All objects are fixed in place except the wooden latch on the entrance door, which can be moved to open or close the hall. The layout preserves the medieval tavern’s authentic spatial arrangement with stone walls, creaking floors, and ample room for movement around the counter and hearth.

STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY
The Stone Hearth Tavern is a rectangular stone hall measuring eighty feet east‑west and sixty feet north‑south, with thick stone walls that creak under the wooden floor. The south wall hosts the main entrance: a single back door centered on the wall, its wooden latch protruding from the rear to allow inward swing. A clear aisle at least twelve feet wide runs from this doorway straight north toward the hall’s northern edge. Along the east side of the hall sits the counter—a three‑foot‑wide bar that stretches twenty feet in length. Its centreline lies ten feet north of the south wall and fifteen feet west of the eastern wall, leaving ample space for patrons to move around. The polished tables are set along this counter; each is four by four feet and fixed at their positions. Near the back end of the counter a high bar stool stands beside a shelf of crystal cups that rises six feet tall and three feet deep. On the west wall sits the hearth, occupying a ten‑by‑twelve‑foot stone area centred fifteen feet northward from the south wall. Beside this hearth an upright barrel—eight feet high with a four‑foot diameter—is positioned on the floor. The rear northwest corner of the hall holds a single back table measuring five by five feet; it is placed three feet north‑west of the counter’s centreline, ensuring no overlap with other furniture or walls. Above each polished table and above the back table hangs a lantern suspended six feet from the floor, providing illumination for patrons. All objects are fixed in place except the wooden latch on the entrance door, which can be moved to open or close the hall. The layout preserves the medieval tavern’s authentic spatial arrangement with stone walls, creaking floors, and ample room for movement around the counter and hearth.

RAW SCENE
The polished table remains gleaming beneath the lantern’s glow.  
A green‑skinned goblin leans over the counter clutching a chipped mug with both hands.  
Amy stands beside him on the east side of the hall, her rough‑spun linen tunic brushing the wooden floor as she takes a short stride toward the hearth.  
She lifts a handful of liquid from the barrel that sits near the west wall and tilts it toward the goblin’s mug.  
The liquid pours into the chipped rim, filling the mug to its brim while the goblin watches attentively.

Validate only whether the sequential physical actions in one timed RAW SCENE can visibly occur within the time available between its timestamps as one continuous shot. Read each consecutive timed micro-beat literally. Reject only obvious time compression that would force a hidden cut, teleport, skipped prerequisite, or instantaneous relocation/manipulation. Pay special attention when a subject or object must enter, cross meaningful space, reach a new established position, sit or stand, acquire or position a prop, or complete multiple dependent physical steps before the next timestamp. Also inspect multiple verbs inside one timestamp: simultaneous motions may share a timestamp, but a sequential dependency chain such as opening/unlatching a barrier, crossing it, closing it, and securing it cannot be treated as one instantaneous action. Reject when that chain would require hidden time or a cut instead of being split across executable timestamps. Do not impose a fixed minimum interval: simple gestures may be quick and nearby actions may need little time. Camera movement does not erase the time required for subjects or objects to move. Judge only timing feasibility, not prose style, camera taste, story choices, or whether an invented detail was necessary. Return exactly one JSON object with boolean valid and string issue. The Stone Hearth Tavern is a rectangular stone hall measuring eighty feet east‑west and sixty feet north‑south, with thick stone walls that creak under the wooden floor. The south wall hosts the main entrance: a single back door centered on the wall, its wooden latch protruding from the rear to allow inward swing. A clear aisle at least twelve feet wide runs from this doorway straight north toward the hall’s northern edge. Along the east side of the hall sits the counter—a three‑foot‑wide bar that stretches twenty feet in length. Its centreline lies ten feet north of the south wall and fifteen feet west of the eastern wall, leaving ample space for patrons to move around. The polished tables are set along this counter; each is four by four feet and fixed at their positions. Near the back end of the counter a high bar stool stands beside a shelf of crystal cups that rises six feet tall and three feet deep. On the west wall sits the hearth, occupying a ten‑by‑twelve‑foot stone area centred fifteen feet northward from the south wall. Beside this hearth an upright barrel—eight feet high with a four‑foot diameter—is positioned on the floor. The rear northwest corner of the hall holds a single back table measuring five by five feet; it is placed three feet north‑west of the counter’s centreline, ensuring no overlap with other furniture or walls. Above each polished table and above the back table hangs a lantern suspended six feet from the floor, providing illumination for patrons. All objects are fixed in place except the wooden latch on the entrance door, which can be moved to open or close the hall. STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY
The Stone Hearth Tavern is a rectangular stone hall measuring eighty feet east‑west and sixty feet north‑south, with thick stone walls that creak under the wooden floor. The south wall hosts the main entrance: a single back door centered on the wall, its wooden latch protruding from the rear to allow inward swing. A clear aisle at least twelve feet wide runs from this doorway straight north toward the hall’s northern edge. Along the east side of the hall sits the counter—a three‑foot‑wide bar that stretches twenty feet in length. Its centreline lies ten feet north of the south wall and fifteen feet west of the eastern wall, leaving ample space for patrons to move around. The polished tables are set along this counter; each is four by four feet and fixed at their positions. Near the back end of the counter a high bar stool stands beside a shelf of crystal cups that rises six feet tall and three feet deep. On the west wall sits the hearth, occupying a ten‑by‑twelve‑foot stone area centred fifteen feet northward from the south wall. Beside this hearth an upright barrel—eight feet high with a four‑foot diameter—is positioned on the floor. The rear northwest corner of the hall holds a single back table measuring five by five feet; it is placed three feet north‑west of the counter’s centreline, ensuring no overlap with other furniture or walls. Above each polished table and above the back table hangs a lantern suspended six feet from the floor, providing illumination for patrons. All objects are fixed in place except the wooden latch on the entrance door, which can be moved to open or close the hall. The layout preserves the medieval tavern’s authentic spatial arrangement with stone walls, creaking floors, and ample room for movement around the counter and hearth.

RAW SCENE
The liquid splashes into the chipped rim as the goblin watches attentively.

At 00:01.700, the beautiful female elf steps through the south entrance with silver hair streaming down her shoulders; she pauses at the aisle’s midpoint, glances over toward the goblin who is still holding his mug on the counter, and smiles faintly.

She then strides northward toward the back table in the northwest corner of the hall. By 00:03.000 she has reached the edge of the square wooden table, sits down with a gentle thud of her boots on the creaking floor, and settles into a chair beside it.

Amy, standing by the bar counter, lifts a crystal chalice from its shelf next to the high bar stool. She pours steaming liquid from the barrel beside the hearth into the chalice for the elf, catching the steam in the air as it rises toward the lanterns above the tables.

The elf accepts the chalice with both hands, nodding her head appreciatively; Amy sets the chalice on the wood of the back table and gives a warm, grateful smile before turning to attend another patron.

The Stone Hearth Tavern is a rectangular stone hall measuring eighty feet east‑west and sixty feet north‑south, with thick stone walls that creak under the wooden floor. The south wall hosts the main entrance: a single back door centered on the wall, its wooden latch protruding from the rear to allow inward swing. On the west wall sits the hearth, occupying a ten‑by‑twelve‑foot stone area centred fifteen feet northward from the south wall. Beside this hearth an upright barrel—eight feet high with a four‑foot diameter—is positioned on the floor. The rear northwest corner of the hall holds a single back table measuring five by five feet; it is placed three feet north‑west of the counter’s centreline, ensuring no overlap with other furniture or walls. Above each polished table and above the back table hangs a lantern suspended six feet from the floor, providing illumination for patrons. All objects are fixed in place except the wooden latch on the entrance door, which can be moved to open or close the hall. STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY
The Stone Hearth Tavern is a rectangular stone hall measuring eighty feet east‑west and sixty feet north‑south, with thick stone walls that creak under the wooden floor. The south wall hosts the main entrance: a single back door centered on the wall, its wooden latch protruding from the rear to allow inward swing. A clear aisle at least twelve feet wide runs from this doorway straight north toward the hall’s northern edge. Along the east side of the hall sits the counter—a three‑foot‑wide bar that stretches twenty feet in length. Its centreline lies ten feet north of the south wall and fifteen feet west of the eastern wall, leaving ample space for patrons to move around. The polished tables are set along this counter; each is four by four feet and fixed at their positions. Near the back end of the counter a high bar stool stands beside a shelf of crystal cups that rises six feet tall and three feet deep. On the west wall sits the hearth, occupying a ten‑by‑twelve‑foot stone area centred fifteen feet northward from the south wall. Beside this hearth an upright barrel—eight feet high with a four‑foot diameter—is positioned on the floor. The rear northwest corner of the hall holds a single back table measuring five by five feet; it is placed three feet north‑west of the counter’s centreline, ensuring no overlap with other furniture or walls. Above each polished table and above the back table hangs a lantern suspended six feet from the floor, providing illumination for patrons. All objects are fixed in place except the wooden latch on the entrance door, which can be moved to open or close the hall. The layout preserves the medieval tavern’s authentic spatial arrangement with stone walls, creaking floors, and ample room for movement around the counter and hearth.

RAW SCENE
The liquid splashes into the chipped rim as the goblin watches attentively.

At 00:01.700, the beautiful female elf steps through the south entrance with silver hair streaming down her shoulders; she pauses at the aisle’s midpoint, glances over toward the goblin who is still holding his mug on the counter, and smiles faintly.

She then strides northward toward the back table in the northwest corner of the hall. By 00:02.500 she lifts her boot and steps onto the square wooden table’s edge, placing weight on its surface.

By 00:03.000 she sits down with a gentle thud of boots on the creaking floor, settling at its side.

Amy, standing by the bar counter, lifts the crystal chalice she had set back onto its shelf next to the high bar stool. She pours steaming liquid from the barrel beside the hearth into the chalice for the elf, catching the steam in the air as it rises toward the lanterns above the tables.

The elf accepts the chalice with both hands, nodding her head appreciatively; Amy sets the chalice on the wood of the back table and gives a warm, grateful smile before turning to attend another patron.

STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY
The Stone Hearth Tavern is a rectangular stone hall measuring eighty feet east‑west and sixty feet north‑south, with thick stone walls that creak under the wooden floor. The south wall hosts the main entrance: a single back door centered on the wall, its wooden latch protruding from the rear to allow inward swing. A clear aisle at least twelve feet wide runs from this doorway straight north toward the hall’s northern edge. Along the east side of the hall sits the counter—a three‑foot‑wide bar that stretches twenty feet in length. Its centreline lies ten feet north of the south wall and fifteen feet west of the eastern wall, leaving ample space for patrons to move around. The polished tables are set along this counter; each is four by four feet and fixed at their positions. Near the back end of the counter a high bar stool stands beside a shelf of crystal cups that rises six feet tall and three feet deep. On the west wall sits the hearth, occupying a ten‑by‑twelve‑foot stone area centred fifteen feet northward from the south wall. Beside this hearth an upright barrel—eight feet high with a four‑foot diameter—is positioned on the floor. The rear northwest corner of the hall holds a single back table measuring five by five feet; it is placed three feet north‑west of the counter’s centreline, ensuring no overlap with other furniture or walls. Above each polished table and above the back table hangs a lantern suspended six feet from the floor, providing illumination for patrons. All objects are fixed in place except the wooden latch on the entrance door, which can be moved to open or close the hall. The layout preserves the medieval tavern’s authentic spatial arrangement with stone walls, creaking floors, and ample room for movement around the counter and hearth.

The elf accepts the chalice with both hands, nodding her head appreciatively; Amy sets the chalice on the wood of the back table and gives a warm, grateful smile before turning to attend another patron.

STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY
The Stone Hearth Tavern is a rectangular stone hall measuring eighty feet east‑west and sixty feet north‑south, with thick stone walls that creak under the wooden floor. The south wall hosts the main entrance: a single back door centered on the wall, its wooden latch protruding from the rear to allow inward swing. A clear aisle at least twelve feet wide runs from this doorway straight north toward the hall’s northern edge. Along the east side of the hall sits the counter—a three‑foot‑wide bar that stretches twenty feet in length. Its centreline lies ten feet north of the south wall and fifteen feet west of the eastern wall, leaving ample space for patrons to move around. The polished tables are set along this counter; each is four by four feet and fixed at their positions. Near the back end of the counter a high bar stool stands beside a shelf of crystal cups that rises six feet tall and three feet deep. On the west wall sits the hearth, occupying a ten‑by‑twelve‑foot stone area centred fifteen feet northward from the south wall. Beside this hearth an upright barrel—eight feet high with a four‑foot diameter—is positioned on the floor. The rear northwest corner of the hall holds a single back table measuring five by five feet; it is placed three feet north‑west of the counter’s centreline, ensuring no overlap with other furniture or walls. Above each polished table and above the back table hangs a lantern suspended six feet from the floor, providing illumination for patrons. All objects are fixed in place except the wooden latch on the entrance door, which can be moved to open or close the hall. The layout preserves the medieval tavern’s authentic spatial arrangement with stone walls, creaking floors, and ample room for movement around the counter and hearth.

RAW SCENE
The elf steps through the south doorway at 00 :01.700, silver hair falling over her shoulders as she lands on the creaking wooden floor.  
She walks northward along the aisle, glancing briefly toward the goblin leaning over the counter with his chipped mug, who watches her approach attentively.  
By 00 :02.500 the elf reaches the back table in the northwest corner of the hall and leans onto its edge before sitting down on the floor beside it.  
Amy, standing near the hearth, lifts a crystal chalice from the shelf by the high bar stool, pours steaming liquid into it, then gently places the chalice onto the wooden surface of the back table.  
The elf nods her head in thanks as Amy sets the cup down and steps away to serve herself.

The Stone Hearth Tavern is a rectangular stone hall measuring eighty feet east‑west and sixty feet north‑south, with thick stone walls that creak under the wooden floor. The south wall hosts the main entrance: a single back door centered on the wall, its wooden latch protruding from the rear to allow inward swing. A clear aisle at least twelve feet wide runs from this doorway straight north toward the hall’s northern edge. Along the east side of the hall sits the counter—a three‑foot‑wide bar that stretches twenty feet in length. Its centreline lies ten feet north of the south wall and fifteen feet west of the eastern wall, leaving ample space for patrons to move around. The polished tables are set along this counter; each is four by four feet and fixed at their positions. Near the back end of the counter a high bar stool stands beside a shelf of crystal cups that rises six feet tall and three feet deep. On the west wall sits the hearth, occupying a ten‑by‑twelve‑foot stone area centred fifteen feet northward from the south wall. Beside this hearth an upright barrel—eight feet high with a four‑foot diameter—is positioned on the floor. The rear northwest corner of the hall holds a single back table measuring five by five feet; it is placed three feet north‑west of the counter’s centreline, ensuring no overlap with other furniture or walls. Above each polished table and above the back table hangs a lantern suspended six feet from the floor, providing illumination for patrons. All objects are fixed in place except the wooden latch on the entrance door, which can be moved to open or close the hall. The layout preserves the medieval tavern’s authentic spatial arrangement with stone walls, creaking floors, and ample room for movement around the counter and hearth.

STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY
The Stone Hearth Tavern is a rectangular stone hall measuring eighty feet east‑west and sixty feet north‑south, with thick stone walls that creak under the wooden floor. The south wall hosts the main entrance: a single back door centered on the wall, its wooden latch protruding from the rear to allow inward swing. A clear aisle at least twelve feet wide runs from this doorway straight north toward the hall’s northern edge. Along the east side of the hall sits the counter—a three‑foot‑wide bar that stretches twenty feet in length. Its centreline lies ten feet north of the south wall and fifteen feet west of the eastern wall, leaving ample space for patrons to move around. The polished tables are set along this counter; each is four by four feet and fixed at their positions. Near the back end of the counter a high bar stool stands beside a shelf of crystal cups that rises six feet tall and three feet deep. On the west wall sits the hearth, occupying a ten‑by‑twelve‑foot stone area centred fifteen feet northward from the south wall. Beside this hearth an upright barrel—eight feet high with a four‑foot diameter—is positioned on the floor. The rear northwest corner of the hall holds a single back table measuring five by five feet; it is placed three feet north‑west of the counter’s centreline, ensuring no overlap with other furniture or walls. Above each polished table and above the back table hangs a lantern suspended six feet from the floor, providing illumination for patrons. All objects are fixed in place except the wooden latch on the entrance door, which can be moved to open or close the hall. The layout preserves the medieval tavern’s authentic spatial arrangement with stone walls, creaking floors, and ample room for movement around the counter and hearth.
